# Test cases

Every automated test in the repository, in the module's test-case format: one row per test, grouped by
backlog story.

**Running them**

```
npm test                                     # from the repo root — all frontend suites
npm test -- --testPathPattern EventRequestForm   # one suite
npm run test:backend                         # the backend's own suite (node's test runner)
npm run test:coverage --workspace frontend   # with coverage; fails below 100%
npm run test:coverage --workspace backend    # likewise (Node 22.8+)
npm run test:integration                     # emulator tests (*.integration.js); needs Java
```

**Test case IDs.** Each test carries one ID at the start of its name, and no two tests share one:
TC-0001 to TC-2515 (2476 tests).
- IDs run in backlog-story order, A1 to L4, so one story's cases sit together. Tests that belong to no single
  story come last.
- A parameterised test (`test.each`) is one test case.
- IDs are never reused. A test deleted with the code it covered leaves a gap.
- The tests added by PR #23 (data access split into modules) are not numbered or listed here yet.
- The IDs were renumbered on 31 Oct 2026. Earlier numbers had been reused across files, given letter suffixes,
  or written in other formats such as `A6-TC8` and `K3-RT5`. [`test-case-id-map.csv`](test-case-id-map.csv)
  maps every earlier ID to its new one.

To add a test, give it the next free ID after TC-2515, and add its row to its story's table below.

**Columns.**
- **Pre-Condition:** the state the test sets up before it acts, such as who is signed in, the records it
  starts from, the mocked responses and a fixed clock.
  - Unit and functional tests run on in-memory fakes and mocks (`backend/testing/fakeFirestore.js`, mocked
    `lib/firebase` and API modules), so they need no emulator, network or seeded project.
  - Only the `*.integration.js` tests need the Firestore emulator, and their pre-condition says so.
- **Test Steps and Expected Result:** what the test does, with its inputs, and what it asserts.
  - 567 rows keep the detail written for them before the renumbering.
  - 0 were written from each test's code on 31 Oct 2026.
- **Test Scenario:** the group the test sits in, or the story.
- **Test file:** named at the end of Remarks.
- **Remarks:** the story and acceptance criteria, then the scenario kind (normal, boundary, conflict or
  failure). Only a test that declares its kind in its name or in a `describe` block gets one; the rest say
  *unlabelled*. No kind was guessed from wording.
- **Actual Result and status:** recorded from the unit and functional run on 31 Oct 2026.
  - Every one of those tests passed, at 100% statements, branches, functions and lines.
  - The 12 emulator tests (`*.integration.js`) were not part of that run, and are marked Not Executed.

## Coverage, and what the audit found

The unit and functional tests cover each module for **normal**, **boundary**, **conflict** and **failure**
scenarios, at **100% statements, branches, functions and lines**. Integration and end-to-end tests come
later.

**Enforced in CI.** `npm run test:coverage` in either workspace fails below 100%.
- **Files excluded from coverage:** only the bootstrap ones.
  - Frontend: `src/index.js`, `src/reportWebVitals.js`, `src/setupTests.js`, `src/lib/firebase.js` and
    `src/sandbox/**`.
  - Backend: `src/lib/firebaseAdmin.js`, plus each service's Firebase Admin wiring, which is marked
    `node:coverage disable`.
- **Guards kept but not covered:** a branch that cannot be reached from the screen but is kept as a guard
  (such as a double-submit check behind a disabled button) carries an `istanbul ignore` comment saying why.

**Root `test.js` retired (30 Oct 2026).** It tested about 70 copies of rules defined inside the file itself,
so it proved nothing about the product. Each of its suites was moved onto the real module, or dropped where
the copied behaviour was never built.

**Defects and gaps found.** Only D-01 is fixed. No test pins the wrong behaviour of the others.

| Ref | Where | Problem | Story |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| D-01 | `features/events/components/DecisionSummary.js` | **Fixed.** While venues were still loading, the organiser saw "To be confirmed" instead of "Loading…" (TC-0285). | B10 |
| D-02 | `features/events/reviewApi.js:202` | If someone in the event's history has a profile with no name, the whole event page fails to load. | D1 AC2, C3 |
| D-03 | `domain/eventRequest.js:155` | Registration may open and close at the same instant, although the message says close must be after open. | B5 |
| D-04 | `domain/safetyAndNotices.js` `flagArrangements` | On Reject or Request changes, a booking that is already Rejected is flagged and its Venue Staff notified. | L4 AC2–AC3 |
| D-05 | `features/safety/safetyApi.js` send transaction | Only the coordinator is re-checked, not the status, so an event cancelled mid-send could still reach Safety Review unless the rules refuse it. | L1 AC1 |
| D-06 | `features/notifications/NotificationInbox.js:32` | When an event lookup fails, the error is not caught, so its rows stay on "Loading event…". | J1 |
| D-07 | `features/venues/venuesApi.js:456` | A venue with no recorded capacity passes the client capacity check. | E8 AC1 |
| D-08 | `features/venues/venuesApi.js:23` | Clash overlap times computed on the client are Date objects; stored ones are ISO text. | F10 |
| D-09 | `frontend/src/domain/eventCompletion.mjs:15` | `canCompleteEvent(null, …)` throws instead of returning false. The backend never passes null. | D5 |
| G-01 | Event Coordinator Lead | The Lead has no Notifications screen, so the `UNASSIGNED_EVENT` notices are written but never seen. This conflicts with A6 AC4 and needs a team decision. | J8 AC2–AC5 |
| G-02 | Safety Officer notices | No "View" link to the event's safety review. | J9 AC2 |
| G-03 | Venue booking routing | Requests go to the venue's single owner. There is no round-robin and no unassigned booking queue. | F2 AC3, AC5 |
| G-04 | Rejection | Nothing rejects an event automatically because it cannot be fulfilled. | C7 AC4 |
| G-05 | Account forms | No client-side email format rule beyond the browser's `type=email`. | A2 AC6 |

**B9 assumption.** For B9 AC2, an accessibility section counts as answered when its Yes/No question has an
explicit No; an untouched question does not count. This is recorded as an open question in `CLAUDE.md`.

**Unused code retired.** These were called only by the retired `test.js`. They were removed together with
the tests that exercised them, which leaves gaps in the IDs:
- in `domain/venueBooking.js`: `assignVenueStaff`, `unassignedBookingQueue`, `approvedConflicts`,
  `unavailabilityConflicts`, `recordVenueUnavailability` and `searchBlockedByUnavailability`. The F2
  round-robin and unassigned booking queue (G-03) were never wired in.
- in `domain/safetyAndNotices.js`: `markNotificationRead`, `unreadCount`, `confirmEvent`,
  `passedSafetyCheckAfterPlanning`, `safetyReviewFacts` and `NOT_RECORDED`. Production uses the versions in
  `domain/notifications.js`, `domain/safetyReview.js` and `features/safety/safetyApi.js`.
- in `domain/accounts.js`: `resolveRole`, `isStaffEmail` and `emailDomain`. Registration sets the role
  directly (Organiser or Attendee), and `firestore.rules` stops a user changing it. With these gone,
  `REACT_APP_STAFF_EMAIL_DOMAINS` is no longer read.
